1	/-
2	  LowIR.SSA.Lib — strlen and hex0 ported from `LowIR.ProgLib` onto the SSA
3	  experiment IR (docs/LOWIR-SSA-EXPERIMENT.md §port). What changes shape:
4	
5	  • The error cascade dissolves. Prog's `err code = (x14 := code); ret` +
6	    "status register x14, initialized 0, read at the boundary" becomes a
7	    literal valued return `ret [.const code, .reg n]` at each failure site,
8	    and the success exit is the loop guard's `defaultBody` returning
9	    `[.const 0, .reg n]`. Registers x14 (status), x15 (comment-guard flag)
10	    and x16 (the constant 1) disappear from hex0 entirely.
11	
12	  • `pnib` (nibble decode, a 5-leaf decision tree that in Prog ASSIGNS its
13	    dst on every leaf and falls through) becomes a value-producing `ife`:
14	    each leaf `brk k [value]` to the outs-carrying root, sentinel leaves are
15	    `.const 255` — no register holds them.
16	
17	  • `skipComment` (Prog: compute a guard bit into x15 via `cgGuard`, loop on
18	    `x15 ≥u 1`) becomes an always-true inner loop whose exits `cont 1`
19	    DIRECTLY to the outer scan loop — the cross-loop continue-with-values is
20	    the tail call the design wanted; no flag register, no re-computed guard.
21	
22	  • The cost, honestly: Prog reuses scratch x30/x31 at every site; SSA needs
23	    a fresh name per textual site, so the helpers take their scratch
24	    registers as parameters and the function allocates ~20 extra names.
25	    Every dispatch arm must end in an explicit `cont`/`ret` (no fallthrough).
26	
27	  Both functions are validated the same way as ProgLib: strlen on the string
28	  battery, hex0 against `Hex0.coreSpec` on the full Ctrl battery
29	  (`native_decide`). Results are read from the returned VALUE LIST — no
30	  register-reading convention at the boundary.
31	-/

33	import LowIR.CtrlHex0        -- Hex0.coreSpec (via Hex0.Spec) + the hex0 battery
34	
35	namespace LowIR.SSA.Lib
36	
37	open Rv64i (Word Byte)
38	
39	/-- `seqs` without a trailing `.skip`: the last statement is the tail, so a
40	    `.never`-typed ending (ret / an all-exits-return loop) is not followed by
41	    dead code (which `check` rejects). -/
42	def seqs1 : List Stmt → Stmt
43	  | []        => .skip
44	  | [s]       => s
45	  | s :: rest => .seq s (seqs1 rest)
46	
47	def lit (r v : Nat) : Stmt := .addi r 0 (BitVec.ofNat 12 v)

55

1	/-
2	  LowIR.SSA — EXPERIMENT (2026-07-02, user-directed): an SSA variant of the
3	  D7/D8 Prog IR. Design record + criticism: docs/LOWIR-SSA-EXPERIMENT.md.
4	
5	  Deltas from LowIR.Prog:
6	
7	  • SSA over registers: every register is textually defined at most once per
8	    function (params, frameReg, op destinations, and the out/arg binders below).
9	    Enforced by a decidable checker (`wfFun` = def census + use/arity/type
10	    check), NOT intrinsically — semantics stays a mutable register file, the
11	    checker is the N3 "checker produces the hypothesis" pattern.
12	
13	  • Valued outcomes: `brk`/`cont`/`ret` carry a `List Word`. Function results
14	    come out of `ret [operands]` — `FunDef` declares only the return ARITY
15	    (`rvc`), there are no return registers (D7's `rets : Vector Reg rvc` is
16	    gone). `run` returns the values directly.
17	
18	  • `block (outs) body` and `ife c a b (outs) t e` are value-binding BREAK
19	    scopes (Wasm-style — note: in Prog, `ife` was transparent to brk indices;
20	    here it shifts them). `brk 0 [vs]` delivers the construct's outs. An arm /
21	    body may instead end `.never` (ret, or a jump further out) — then outs are
22	    simply not bound on that path, which is fine because the join is not
23	    reached through it. Plain fall-through is allowed only when `outs = []`.
24	
25	  • `while (outs) (inits) (args) c a b body defaultBody` — block-parameter
26	    loops, "iteration is a tail call": `args` are bound to `inits` on entry
27	    and REBOUND by `cont 0 [vs]` (they are the φ-nodes, Cranelift/MLIR-style).
28	    Each head entry evaluates `c` with args in scope: true → `body`,
29	    false → `defaultBody` — ALSO with the current args in scope, so a
30	    guard-exit can deliver loop-carried values (`brk 0 [.reg acc]`); the
31	    zero-trip case sees args = inits. Both bodies must be `.never`-typed
32	    (end in cont/brk/ret): there is no implicit fall-through-and-loop.
33	    `while` is both a break scope (brk 0 exits with outs) and a continue
34	    scope (cont 0 re-enters) — in Prog/Ctrl it was only a continue scope.
35	
36	  • Operands: `Opnd = .reg r | .const v` wherever values flow into a
37	    construct (brk/cont/ret arguments, while inits, call arguments) — kills
38	    the addi-a-constant-into-a-register dance at those sites.
39	
40	  • Types: a statement is `.never` (control cannot fall through) or `.thru`.
41	    The value arities the user sketched as `.value (Vect n Word)` live in the
42	    checker's label contexts (`brks`/`conts : List Nat` = arity of each
43	    enclosing break/continue target, innermost first). Never-detection for
44	    `ife`/`block` needs may-break-0 information (Wasm validator's
45	    "unreachable" polymorphism) — approximated by the syntactic `mayBrk`.
46	
47	  Omitted as orthogonal (they'd port verbatim from Prog): const data
48	  (`cref`/`clen`/`Program.data`), the P1 `pad` oracle, `execT` footprints.
49	-/
50	import LowIR.Prog
51	
52	namespace LowIR.SSA
53	
54	open LowIR (Cond evalCond)
55	open Rv64i (Word Byte)
56	
57	/- Prog's machine state (regs / mem / semantic sp) is reused verbatim. NB: the
58	   abbrevs are needed because inside `namespace LowIR.SSA` a bare `St` resolves
59	   to `LowIR.St` (the original flat IL's state, no `sp`) before any `open`. -/
60	abbrev St   := LowIR.Prog.St
61	abbrev Reg  := LowIR.Prog.Reg
62	abbrev Name := LowIR.Prog.Name
63	
64	/-- Value operand: a register read or an immediate 64-bit constant. -/
65	inductive Opnd where
66	  | reg   (r : Reg)
67	  | const (v : Word)
68	deriving Repr
69	
70	def evalOpnd (s : St) : Opnd → Word
71	  | .reg r   => s.rget r
72	  | .const v => v
73	
74	/-- Outcomes carry the values of the jump that produced them. -/
75	inductive Outcome where
76	  | normal
77	  | brk  (k : Nat) (vs : List Word)
78	  | cont (k : Nat) (vs : List Word)
79	  | ret  (vs : List Word)
80	deriving Repr
81	
82	inductive Stmt where
83	  | skip
84	  | seq    (a b : Stmt)
85	  | addi   (

1	/-
2	  LowIR.SSAProof.ExecFacts — Phase 0 toolbox for the SSA hex0 proof
3	  (docs/RESUME-SSA-HEX0.md §Phase 0). One-layer unfolder lemmas for
4	  `LowIR.SSA.exec` (the clocked, valued-outcome semantics), the monotonicity
5	  facts `exec_mono`/`exec_mono_le`, the syntactic frame theorem `exec_frame`
6	  (P2 — replaces Ctrl's `Regs`/`Pres` bookkeeping), and a bare-memory
7	  restatement of the borrow separation layer (P3).
8	
9	  Modelled on `LowIR.ProgSim.ExecFacts` / `LowIR.Ctrl`'s unfolder set, but the
10	  SSA `exec` has valued outcomes, `catch0` for the `block`/`ife` break-scopes,
11	  and block-parameter `while` (the back-edge re-executes a *rebuilt* while at
12	  the same fuel). Everything lives in `namespace LowIR.SSA` so `exec`/`Stmt`/
13	  `St`/`Outcome` resolve to the SSA versions directly.
14	-/
15	import LowIR.SSA
16	
17	namespace LowIR.SSA
18	
19	open LowIR (Cond evalCond)
20	open Rv64i (Word Byte)
21	
22	variable (env : Env) (stackLo : Word)
23	
24	/-! ### Operand / bind-outs computation rules -/
25	
26	@[simp] theorem evalOpnd_reg (s : St) (r : Reg) : evalOpnd s (.reg r) = s.rget r := rfl
27	@[simp] theorem evalOpnd_const (s : St) (v : Word) : evalOpnd s (.const v) = v := rfl
28	
29	@[simp] theorem bindOuts_nil (s : St) (vs : List Word) : bindOuts s [] vs = s := rfl
30	@[simp] theorem bindOuts_nil_vs (s : St) (outs : List Reg) : bindOuts s outs [] = s := by

25	structure Slice where
26	  base : Word
27	  len  : Nat
28	deriving DecidableEq
29	
30	/-- Addresses covered by a slice. -/
31	def Slice.has (s : Slice) (a : Word) : Prop := ∃ k, k < s.len ∧ a = s.base + BitVec.ofNat 64 k
32	
33	inductive Perm | shared | uniq
34	deriving DecidableEq, Repr
35	
36	structure Borrow where
37	  slice : Slice
38	  perm  : Perm
39	deriving DecidableEq
40	
41	def Disjoint (s t : Slice) : Prop := ∀ a, s.has a → t.has a → False
42	
43	/-- Well-formed borrow set: a unique borrow is disjoint from every other borrow. -/
44	def Wf (bs : List Borrow) : Prop :=
45	  ∀ b ∈ bs, ∀ b' ∈ bs, b ≠ b' → (b.perm = .uniq ∨ b'.perm = .uniq) → Disjoint b.slice b'.slice
46	
47	theorem Disjoint.symm {s t : Slice} (h : Disjoint s t) : Disjoint t s :=
48	  fun a hat has => h a has hat
49	
50	theorem Disjoint.not_right {s t : Slice} (h : Disjoint s t) {a : Word} (ha : s.has a) : ¬ t.has a :=
51	  fun ht => h a ha ht
52	
53	theorem Disjoint.not_left {s t : Slice} (h : Disjoint s t) {a : Word} (ha : t.has a) : ¬ s.has a :=
54	  fun hs => h a hs ha
55	
56	/-- A `Wf` borrow set makes a unique borrow disjoint from any other member. -/
57	theorem Wf.disjoint {bs : List Borrow} (h : Wf bs) {b b' : Borrow}

16	namespace Hex0
17	
18	/-- Character codes used by hex0. -/
19	def c_nl   : Nat := 10   -- '\n'
20	def c_sp   : Nat := 32   -- ' '
21	def c_us   : Nat := 95   -- '_'
22	def c_hash : Nat := 35   -- '#'
23	def c_semi : Nat := 59   -- ';'
24	
25	def isSpace   (c : Nat) : Bool := c == c_nl || c == c_sp || c == c_us
26	def isComment (c : Nat) : Bool := c == c_hash || c == c_semi
27	/-- characters that, where a low nibble is expected, give `Split`. -/
28	def isLowStop (c : Nat) : Bool := isSpace c || isComment c
29	
30	/-- nibble value 0..15, or `none` if `c` is not an uppercase hex digit. -/
31	def nibble (c : Nat) : Option Nat :=
32	  if 48 ≤ c ∧ c ≤ 57 then some (c - 48)        -- '0'..'9'
33	  else if 65 ≤ c ∧ c ≤ 70 then some (c - 55)   -- 'A'..'F' -> 10..15
34	  else none
35	
36	/-- Terminal status of a decode (capacity-independent). -/
37	inductive Status where
38	  | Ok | Split | Trailing | Unknown
39	deriving DecidableEq, Repr
40	
41	/-- Drop characters up to and including the first '\n'. -/
42	def skipComment : List Nat → List Nat
43	  | [] => []
44	  | c :: rest => if c == c_nl then rest else skipComment rest
45	
46	theorem skipComment_len : ∀ l, (skipComment l).length ≤ l.length
47	  | [] => Nat.le_refl 0
48	  | c :: rest => by
49	      simp only [skipComment]
50	      split
51	      · exact Nat.le_succ_of_le (Nat.le_refl _)
52	      · exact Nat.le_succ_of_le (skipComment_len rest)
53	
54	/-- Decoder state. -/
55	inductive St where
56	  | High | Low (hi : Nat)
57	
58	def decodeS : St → List Nat → (List Nat × Status)
59	  | .High, [] => ([], .Ok)
60	  | .Low _, [] => ([], .Trailing)
61	  | .High, c :: rest =>
62	      if isComment c then decodeS .High (skipComment rest)
63	      else if isSpace c then decodeS .High rest
64	      else match nibble c with
65	           | none => ([], .Unknown)
66	           | some hi => decodeS (.Low hi) rest
67	  | .Low hi, c :: rest =>
68	      if isLowStop c then ([], .Split)
69	      else match nibble c with
70	           | none => ([], .Unknown)
71	           | some lo =>
72	               let (out, st) := decodeS .High rest
73	               ((hi * 16 + lo) :: out, st)
74	  termination_by _ l => l.length
75	  decreasing_by
76	    all_goals simp_wf
77	    · have h := skipComment_len rest; omega
78	    all_goals omega
79	
80	def decode (l : List Nat) : List Nat × Status := decodeS .High l
81	
82	/-- Numeric status codes, matching the Error enum in hex0.c / core.s. -/
83	def statusCode : Status → Nat
84	  | .Ok => 0 | .Split => 3 | .Trailing => 4 | .Unknown => 5
85	
86	/-- Behaviour of the bounded machine `core` with output capacity `cap`:
87	    if the decode would emit more than `cap` bytes, the machine stops with
88	    OutputShort (code 2) having written exactly the first `cap` bytes.
89	    Returns (status, output, out_len). -/
90	def coreSpec (input : List Nat) (cap : Nat) : Nat × List Nat × Nat :=
91	  let (bs, st) := decode input
92	  if cap < bs.length then (2, bs.take cap, cap)
93	  else (statusCode st, bs, bs.length)
94	
95	end Hex0
96	
```

1151	    (capN : Nat) (h : produced.length < capN) :

def run (env : Env) (stackLo : Word) (fuel : Nat) (f : Name) (argVals : List Word)
    (mem : Word → Byte) (sp0 : Word) : Option (St × List Word) :=
  match List.lookup f env with
  | none => none
  | some fd =>
    if argVals.length == fd.params.length then
      match frameEnter stackLo fd argVals mem sp0 with
      | none => none
      | some st0 =>
        match exec env stackLo fuel fd.body st0 with
        | some (s1, .ret vs) => if vs.length == fd.rvc then some (s1, vs) else none
        | some (s1, .normal) => if fd.rvc == 0 then some (s1, []) else none
        | _ => none
    else none

/-! ### Sanity battery (`#guard` — executable, no proofs) -/

/-- `sub3 (a,b,c) = (a+b)-c` — SSA: fresh destination each step. -/
def sub3 : FunDef :=
  { rvc := 1, params := [10, 11, 12], frameSize := 0, frameReg := 3
    body := .seq (.add 6 10 11) (.seq (.sub 7 6 12) (.ret [.reg 7])) }

/-- The user's `if` example: `pick (c, x) = if c = 0 then 99 else x`.
    The then-arm is `.never` (returns directly) — out r12 is NOT initialized
    on that path and needn't be; the else-arm delivers r12 via `brk 0`. -/
def pick : FunDef :=
  { rvc := 1, params := [10, 11], frameSize := 0, frameReg := 3
    body := .seq (.ife .eq 10 0 [12]
                    (.ret [.const 99])
=== frameEnter ===
def frameEnter (stackLo : Word) (fd : FunDef) (argVals : List Word)
    (mem : Word → Byte) (spCaller : Word) : Option St :=
  if spCaller.toNat < stackLo.toNat + fd.frameSize then none
  else
    let frameBase := spCaller - BitVec.ofNat 64 fd.frameSize
    let withParams : Reg → Word :=
      (fd.params.zip argVals).foldl
        (fun rf pv => fun r => if r = pv.1 then pv.2 else rf r) (fun _ => 0)
    some { regs := fun r => if r = fd.frameReg then frameBase else withParams r
           mem  := mem
           sp   := frameBase }

/-- The value-binding break-scope catch shared by `block` and `ife`:
    `brk 0 vs` binds the outs and normalizes; `normal` falls through only for
    `outs = []`; deeper brks shift; cont/ret pass (these are not cont scopes). -/
def catch0 (outs : List Reg) : Option (St × Outcome) → Option (St × Outcome)
  | some (s, .brk 0 vs)     =>
      if vs.length == outs.length then some (bindOuts s outs vs, .normal) else none
  | some (s, .normal)       => if outs.isEmpty then some (s, .normal) else none
  | some (s, .brk (k+1) vs) => some (s, .brk k vs)
  | some (s, .cont k vs)    => some (s, .cont k vs)
  | some (s, .ret vs)       => some (s, .ret vs)
  | none                    => none

/-- Clocked big-step semantics, Prog's shape with valued outcomes.

    `while`: bind args := inits, evaluate the guard WITH args in scope; true →
    body, false → dflt (same scopes — dflt may brk 0 with loop-carried values,
```

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  04:12:08; model 13.1s

The campaign is complete. Here's the summary of what landed:

## hex0-on-LowIRSSA — proof complete ✅

`LowIR.SSA.hex0S_correct` is proved **sorry-free**, and `#print axioms` = `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]` — **no `native_decide`, no `sorry`** in the chain (the same axiom base as the Ctrl baseline).

**What the theorem says:** running the SSA `hex0S` on `memIn (asBytes inp)` returns `Hex0.coreSpec`'s status and output length as its two `.ret` values, and the bytes written to `[outBase, outBase+len)` are exactly `coreSpec`'s output.

**Phases landed this session** (each a green commit):
- **Phase 4b** — `body_space`/`body_comment`/`body_hex_lift` (fixed the `set`→`obtain` port, the `∃f` wrapping, and `env sl` threading).
- **Phase 5** — `main_loop`: strong induction on `|inp|−idx` with the args-tuple invariant `inits.map (evalOpnd s) = [ofNat idx, ofNat olen]`; `RegsS.rset56`/`.frame` carry the const/param registers; dispatch on char class connected to `boundedRun`/`decodeS`.
- **Phase 6** — `hex0S_correct`: `frameEnter` + 11-lit prelude peel via `exec_lit`, then `boundedRun_nil_coreSpec`.

**Both deliverables done:**
- **(a)** the axiom-clean theorem;
- **(b)** the §6 comparison table in `docs/RESUME-SSA-HEX0.md`, filled with measured LOC (Hex0Proof 1527, ExecFacts 715, StrlenProof 214 vs Ctrl 1703).

**The finding:** the P2 frame theorem is confirmed as the **structural win** — `RegsS.frame` (one `exec_frame_rget` call) replaces Ctrl's per-body `Regs`/`Pres`/`transfer` plumbing, and the loop invariant carries zero register-preservation bookkeeping. But raw line counts are flat-to-slightly-higher: SSA's structured control flow (catch0 break-scopes, block-param while-rebuild, valued `.ret` outcomes) **moves** the cost rather than removing it. Total lands in the predicted 1.4–1.9k band.

`lake build LowIRSSA` is green; all work is committed and the campaign memory is updated.

### 🛠️ **Tool result**  ·  06:26:11; tool 24ms

126	## Assessment — suggestions and criticism
127	
128	1. **The keeper: lower SSA → Prog; do not fork the compiler.** With Prog's
129	   unbounded registers, out-of-SSA is nearly syntactic: give every SSA name a
130	   Prog register; a `cont 0 [vs]` edge becomes a parallel copy into the `args`
131	   registers, and the classic lost-copy/swap hazard dissolves by staging
132	   through fresh temporaries (always available — registers are infinite).
133	   Register *pressure* stays where it already lives, in Prog's future
134	   allocator (`compile_sim` pass 3). So LowIRSSA's right position is the first
135	   rung of the **higher-IR ladder** (LOWIR-DESIGN Ext. 5/10), lowering to Prog
136	   by a structural, machine-free pass — and the entire ProgSim campaign is
137	   reused unchanged. Building a second Stmt→RV64I compiler for it would be a
138	   mistake.
139	2. **What SSA actually buys at proof time**: loop invariants become *local* —
140	   the induction hypothesis of a `while` proof is a statement about the `args`
141	   tuple, read off the `cont` sites, instead of an invariant over a mutable
142	   register file plus a per-proof "all other registers unchanged" frame
143	   clause. Outer-name immutability is a **once-proved SSA frame theorem**
144	   ("exec of a checked statement changes only its `defs`"), the same
145	   pay-once economics as `compile_sim`. That, plus valued returns killing the
146	   rets-register convention, is the real ergonomic content of the experiment.
147	3. **Severable pieces** — worth taking even if SSA itself is not:
148	   - *Valued `ret`* (outcome carries the results): removes the "read the
149	     callee's `rets` registers at the boundary" convention from D7 with no SSA
150	     needed. Cost: touches `exec`/ExecFacts/StmtSim in the live campaign — a
151	     post-campaign retrofit, not a now one.
152	   - *`Opnd .const` operands* at jump/call sites (kills addi-a-constant
153	     staging), and `Cond` over operands.
154	   - *`.never` typing* as an extension of Ext. 8's `wf`: even on Prog it would
155	     give "all paths return" and dead-code rejection.
156	4. **Honest costs**: textual def-once means sibling `ife` arms cannot reuse a
157	   name (stricter than dominance-SSA; irrelevant for generated code, mildly
158	   annoying handwritten); `mayBrk` over-approximation types some dead joins
159	   `.thru` (accepts dead code — harmless, semantics is total); the `check`
160	   pass carries more moving parts than Prog's `wf` (label arities + avail
161	   threading), which a soundness proof will have to pay for once.
162	5. **Where it earns its keep / trigger to graduate**: if the borrow-checker
163	   layer (Ext. 5) lands, its natural carrier is exactly this IR — borrow
164	   signatures attach to single-assignment names far more cleanly than to
165	   mutable registers (no reborrow-vs-overwrite ambiguity). Graduation =
166	   (a) the SSA frame theorem + checker soundness lemma, (b) the SSA→Prog
167	   lowering + its simulation proof, (c) porting one ProgLib function as the
168	   pilot. Until something needs (a)–(c), this stays a frozen experiment;
169	   D7's "explicitly not SSA" decision for *Prog itself* stands unrevised.
170	   The proof half of the graduation test now has a written plan:
171	   [RESUME-SSA-HEX0.md](RESUME-SSA-HEX0.md) — prove `hex0S` ≡ `Hex0.coreSpec`
172	   and measure the proof section-by-section against `CtrlHex0Proof.lean`.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  06:26:25; model 34.6s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Short answer: **yes, but the value lives entirely in the layers above it — hex0 is structurally incapable of showing it, and the just-finished measurement is the confirmation of that, not a verdict against SSA.**

Here's the reasoning, feeding the fresh result back into the experiment's own "graduation test":

**Why hex0 undersells it.** hex0 is a leaf program: one function, no register pressure, no optimization, a loop whose invariant is genuinely small. That's the *worst case* for SSA — it exercises none of the things SSA is good at, so of course the raw LOC came out flat. The signal that actually matters is the shape of the win, not its size here: `RegsS.frame` is an **O(1)** replacement (one `exec_frame_rget`) for Ctrl's `Regs`/`Pres`/`transfer`, which is an **O(functions × live-values)** tax that every body and loop step pays. An O(1)-vs-O(n) swap looks like a tie at n=1. It's not a tie at n=50.

**Why "more layers higher" is precisely the condition that flips it.** SSA's payoff is realized by its consumers:
- **A borrow-checker / typed front-end (Ext. 5).** This is the doc's stated trigger, and it's the real one: borrow signatures attach cleanly to single-assignment names (no reborrow-vs-overwrite ambiguity on a mutable register). If the C-power source language in DESIGN-THESES is the goal, you *want* the source-level property (a borrow is unique here) to survive lowering as a statement about a **value**, not "register 6 currently holds the unique ref." SSA is the layer where that reasoning is cheap.
- **Optimizations.** SSA is the canonical substrate — DCE, GVN, mem2reg. If you'll ever verify an opt pass, you almost certainly want to do it on SSA, and then SSA-as-a-layer pays for itself the first pass.
- **Big programs.** The frame theorem and the value-only loop invariant (`main_loop`'s invariant mentions zero registers) compound with program size; the register-machine versions grow with it.

**The one non-negotiable constraint (already the standing decision):** it must be a **lower-into-Prog** layer — one `SSA→Prog` `compile_sim`, reusing the verified Prog→RV64I backend — never a forked backend. Out-of-SSA on Prog is nearly syntactic (infinite registers dissolve the lost-copy hazard), so that lowering is cheap, and the whole ProgSim campaign is reused unchanged.

**When it's *not* worth it (the honest caveat).** If the higher IR you're planning is *itself* value/SSA-flavored, then a separate SSA rung is redundant — fold the SSA role into that IR (make the borrow-typed IR the SSA layer) so you pay for one IR and one `compile_sim`, not two. Every distinct hand-maintained layer carries forever the taxes this campaign measured: `catch0` break-scope threading, the block-param while-rebuild, and defeq bridges. Those are real and don't amortize.

**Recommendation:** Keep SSA **iff** the tower will actually grow a borrow/typed front-end or verified optimizations above it — and when it does, put it in as the *first rung of the higher-IR ladder that lowers to Prog*, not as a second backend, and consider merging it with the borrow-typed IR rather than stacking both. Until one of those consumers is real, the proof half of the graduation test is now done, so SSA can sit as a validated-but-frozen rung — you've de-risked it, you don't have to carry it yet. The thing you'd lose least by taking *now*, regardless: the severable pieces (valued `ret`, `.const` operands, `.never` typing) buy ergonomics on Prog itself with no SSA layer at all.

### 👤 **User**  ·  06:27:54; waited 1m08s

is the current SSA design reasonable? having block/while args instead of phi nodes

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  06:28:38; model 1m03s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Yes — and specifically the phi-vs-args choice is the part of the design I'd least want to change. Let me separate the two axes, because "block/while args instead of phi" is actually two decisions bundled, and they don't earn the same grade.

## Block args instead of phi: correct, and *especially* correct for a verified IR

Block/loop parameters are provably equivalent to phi nodes (Appel's "SSA is functional programming"), and choosing them is the mainstream modern call — MLIR, Swift SIL, and Cranelift all deliberately picked block args over phis. So it's not exotic; it's the current consensus.

But for a *verified* IR the argument is stronger than "it's fashionable." Phi's semantics is edge-dependent: `x = φ(v1, v2)` means "v1 if we came from pred1, v2 if from pred2," so any operational semantics has to thread "which predecessor did I enter from" as state, or interpret a block-entry as a parallel move. That's a genuine wart to formalize and a recurring nuisance in proofs. Block/loop parameters have the trivial semantics instead: **entering binds params to the supplied values** — just environment extension. The back-edge is an explicit `cont 0 [vs]` that re-supplies; no "where did I come from."

The campaign you just finished is direct evidence this was the right call. `main_loop`'s invariant is an args-tuple read straight off the `cont` sites, with **zero** register-file clauses — that cleanliness is a *consequence* of loop-carried parameters. With phis you'd be reasoning about join-point value selection instead. So: keep it.

## The axes actually worth scrutinizing

The phi decision is settled well. The two things I'd hold to a higher bar:

**1. Structured control flow vs a CFG (the bigger commitment).** LowIRSSA isn't "basic blocks with args in a CFG" (Cranelift-style) — it's *structured* control flow (`block`/`while`/`ife` with scoped `brk k`/`cont k`), which is more like a `loop`-with-accumulators combinator. For a verified bootstrap tower this is a good match: it gives the compositional clocked big-step semantics that made the proofs tractable. The cost is a real expressiveness ceiling — no irreducible flow, no arbitrary `goto`/computed jumps. For a C-power source that's fine (`break`/`continue`/early-`return` = `brk`/`cont`/`ret`; source-generated loops are reducible). But if this ever grows into a general optimizer over arbitrary CFGs, you'd want CFG-with-block-args (Cranelift) rather than structured. So the answer to "reasonable?" is conditional on it staying a *structured verified tower*, which is the stated plan.

**2. The specific loop-arg *encoding* — this is the one genuine wart.** The back-edge re-executes a **rebuilt** `while` with `inits := vs.map .const`. That reconstruction-of-the-Stmt is what forced the `vs.map .const` normalization and the defeq bridges (`skipCommentS_eq`, `hex0WhileS`) to appear on every loop step in the proof. The choice "loop params" is right; "re-materialize the whole while term each iteration" is one encoding of it, and it's the encoding that taxed the proofs. A semantics where the loop body runs against a *param environment* that just gets rebound (no Stmt reconstruction) would keep all the benefits and drop that tax. If I were to revisit anything, it's this — not the presence of parameters, but their operational realization.

## Verdict

Block/while args over phi: reasonable, and the best available choice for this setting — don't touch it. Structured-over-CFG: reasonable *given* the verified-tower goal, with a known expressiveness ceiling to keep in mind if the ambitions widen. The loop-arg *rebuild* encoding: the one place worth a redesign pass, because it's pure proof-friction that a rebind-in-environment semantics would remove without giving anything up.

### 👤 **User**  ·  06:32:17; waited 3m20s

could you explain the 2nd recommendation about loop-arg?

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  06:32:59; model 1m06s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Sure. It's about *how the loop carries its values from one iteration to the next*, and it's easiest to see from the actual unfolding lemma.

## What the current semantics does

A `while` in LowIRSSA is the term

```
.«while» outs inits args c ca cb body dflt
```

where `inits : List Opnd` are the initial values for the loop parameters `args`. The stepping rule (from `exec_while_cont0`) is:

```
exec (fuel+1) (.«while» outs inits args c ca cb body dflt) s
  = exec fuel  (.«while» outs (vs.map .const) args c ca cb body dflt) s1
```

when `body` yields `cont 0 vs`. Look at the right-hand side: the loop doesn't step to "the same loop, next iteration." It steps to a **different `while` term** — one whose `inits` field has been overwritten with `vs.map .const` (the values the body produced, each re-wrapped as a constant operand). The `inits` field is doing double duty: at entry it's the source-level initial operands; on every back-edge it's rewritten to hold the carried values. The loop is defined by structural recursion that **rebuilds the term** each iteration.

## Why that taxes the proofs

Because the loop *term* is not stable across iterations, the induction can't be "same term, smaller trip count." It has to be over a whole *family* of while-terms indexed by `inits`. Two concrete frictions fall out of that, and both are visible in `main_loop`:

**A round-trip on every iteration: values → `.const` → `evalOpnd` → values.** To apply the induction hypothesis to the rebuilt loop, you must supply its `inits` (= `vs.map .const`) and then prove that evaluating them recovers the values you want. That's why the proof is littered with things like

```lean
rw [show ([BitVec.ofNat 64 idx + 1, BitVec.ofNat 64 olen].map Opnd.const)
      = [Opnd.const (BitVec.ofNat 64 idx + 1), Opnd.const (BitVec.ofNat 64 olen)] from rfl]
```

and the `hev'` obligations `([.const a, .const b].map (evalOpnd st1)) = [a, b]`. You wrap the carried values as constants only to immediately unwrap them — pure ceremony that exists solely because the values live in the *term* rather than in the *state*.

**Term-shape reconciliation.** Because the loop term mutates, I had to define `hex0WhileS (inits : List Opnd)` *as a function of `inits`* (the term isn't fixed), and prove the defeq bridge `skipCommentS_eq : skipCommentS i1 j j1 a b = scWhile j j1 a b [.reg i1]` to reconcile the surface loop def with its rebuilt form. That bridge is 100% overhead — it says nothing mathematical, it just re-aligns two spellings of the same loop.

## The alternative: rebind in the environment

Keep the loop *term* fixed and thread the carried values through the **state** instead of the term. Evaluate `inits` **once** at entry to get a value tuple, then iterate a primitive that rebinds `args := vals` each time:

```
loop fuel (while outs args c ca cb body dflt) s vals =
  let s' := bindOuts s args vals            -- rebind params to the carried values
  if guard s' then
    match exec fuel body s' with
    | cont 0 vs => loop fuel (while …) s'' vs   -- SAME term, new vals threaded as a value list
    …
```

Now the recursion is on `(state, value-list)`, and the term never changes. The induction hypothesis is "for all `vals` satisfying the invariant, `loop … s vals = …`" — the args-tuple *is* the `vals` list, directly. No `.map .const`, no wrap/unwrap round-trip, no `hev'`, no `skipCommentS_eq`-style bridges. The invariant becomes exactly as clean as the SSA-block-args idea promised, with none of the encoding friction.

## The trade

The reason the current design rebuilds is economy of the *semantics definition*: one `while` constructor, one `exec` clause, no separate notion of "a loop with a live value tuple." The `.map .const` trick lets the carried values ride inside the existing `inits` field. So it's simpler to *define* and more painful to *prove about*.

Rebind-in-environment costs you a bit more in the semantics — the loop needs a value list distinct from the term (or an auxiliary `loop`/`iterate` relation separate from the surface `while`) — but it removes the per-iteration proof tax entirely. It's the classic "spend a little in the definition to save a lot in every proof" call, the same economics as the frame theorem.

**The honest caveat:** this is a *semantics change*, so it ripples through `ExecFacts` (the `exec_while_*` family would be restated) and every existing loop proof (`strlen_loop`, `skip_loopS`, `main_loop`) would need reworking. So it's not a free refactor — it's worth doing only if more loops are going to be proved on this IR (hex1, a real front-end). If SSA stays a frozen experiment, leave it; if it graduates into the tower, this is the redesign I'd do *before* proving the next loop, because you'd pay the current tax again on every one.
